L2395: September 8, 2005
L2396: Protocol JE049 #4102 (PRO endpoint validation protocol) submitted (SN 0013)
L2397: The study was intended to estimate the Minimum Clinically Significant Difference (MCSD) in VAS scores. Patients were asked to score their symptom response using the Verbal Descriptor Scale (VDS), classifying their symptom as “much more,” “a little more,” “about the same,” “a little less,” or “much less” compared to baseline. The MCSD was defined as the VAS change most predictive of the VDS categories of “a little less” and “a little more” for each symptoms. The MO review dated January 26, 2006, and SEALD consult cited several concerns with the protocol:
L2398: • The VDS required more rigorous definition. Incorporation of a temporal element in the VDS makes this measure less reliable.• The patient population in the validation study was not representative of the patients participating in the phase 3 study.• Patient diaries used in Phase 3 studies should be included in the validation protocol.
L2399: Potential labeling limitations for claims supported solely by the VASFebruary 9, 2006Response to Division/SEALD review regarding validation study (SN 015). Reference to other clinical studies that have used a Verbal Descriptor Scale to determine the MCSD in VAS scores.June 1, 2006SEALD review of February 9, 2006 response. Patient diaries and planned subject enrollment deemed acceptable. Concerns raised regarding that the VAS is not a comprehensive measure of HAE symptoms and does not correlate clearly with the proposed indication.June 20, 2006SAP for Study #2103 (FAST-1) submitted via emailThe Statistical review team asked the Applicant to provide the assumed MCSD used to calculate the sample size of N=56 with a 1:1 randomization to icatibant or placebo. The review team also recommended that the Applicant prespecify alternative analyses to be used if the proportional hazards assumption was not supported by the observed data.October 10, 2006Revised SAP submitted (SN 0026)October 13, 2006Proposed trade name application submitted (SN 0027)January 24, 2007Pre-NDA meeting. L2405: 5. CLINICAL STUDIES
L2406: The submission refers to seven clinical studies: two Phase 1 studies in healthy volunteers (1101 and 1102), one Phase 2 pharmacokinetic study to assess the effects of age and gender (1103), one Phase 2 open-label study in HAE patients (2101), two Phase 3 efficacy and safety studies in HAE patients (2102 and 2103), and a non-interventional validation study of the PRO instrument used in the Phase 3 studies (4102).
L2407: The clinical review of efficacy and safety of the proposed product will focus on the two Phase 3 trials and the open-label extensions of these studies. The Phase 2 open-label study in HAE patients and the validation study will be reviewed for additional efficacy
L2408: support. Data from healthy volunteers will also be reviewed, primarily for assessment of safety, with particular focus on Study 1103 which included serial ECG monitoring. The study reports and synopses are appropriately indexed to allow review. A brief overview of the two Phase 3 trials is provided below.
L2409: Reviewer's comment: In prior discussion (February 6, 2007, Teleconference Minutes), the Division informed the Applicant that Study 1103 did not meet the requirements of a thorough QT study. Preliminary review of the data at that time suggested that icatibant did prolong the QT interval. As a result, a thorough QT study was not warranted unless the Applicant chose to refute the Division's assessment.
L2410: Study 2102 (FAST-2)
L2411: • Dates: March 1, 2005 to July 25, 2006, multicenter European study• Design○ Double-blind, double-dummy, randomized, active controlled Phase 3 study in patients with acute attacks of HAE○ Open label treatment with icatibant for patients presenting with laryngeal edema○ Open-label extension phase for all patients• Objective: Assess the efficacy of a single treatment of icatibant compared with oral tranexamic acid on the onset of relief of symptoms during moderate to very severe acute cutaneous and/or abdominal HAE attacks• Treatments (double-dummy design; no placebo arm)○ Icatibant 30 mg SC injection○ Tranexamic acid 1000 mg (2 x 500 mg tablet) every 6- 8 hours for a total of 4 doses (blinded with over-encapsulating)○ Placebo SC injection solution○ Placebo oral tablet• Patients○ Documented Type I or Type II HAE (medical history and/or functional C1-INH &lt;50% normal)○ 18 years of age and older○ N=74 (36 in icatibant treatment group; 38 in tranexamic acid group)
L2412: Reviewer's comment: Although both functional C1-INH and C4 levels were collected, the inclusion criteria were based only on the C1-INH level. C1q levels were not obtained. Although a diagnosis of acquired angioedema (AAE) is an exclusion criterion, it is possible that patients with AAE who have previously been presumed to have HAE may have been enrolled in this study.
L2413: • Study conduct• Initial screening for eligibility and randomization○ Double-blind treatment phase▪ Patient presentation with moderate-to-severe cutaneous and/or abdominal edema no later than 6 hours after the time the
L2414: current attack became moderate in severity (Visual Analogue Scale \geq 30 mm)▪ Hospitalization for a minimum of 12 hours during acute attack, with follow-up on Day 2 and Day 14▪ VAS scoring Q30\text{min} for 1 to 4 hours after initial injection, then 5, 6, 8, 10, and 12 hours post-dose▪ If no symptom relief by 12 hours post-dose, hospitalization extended for an additional 3 hours with additional symptom assessment at 14 hours after injection▪ Rescue medications to be avoided if possible, otherwise documented in the CRF○ Open label treatment with icatibant 30 mg SC for laryngeal edema attack○ Open-label extension phase (OLE)▪ After completion of Visit 4 (Day 14) follow-up of initial attack, patients enrolled in OLE▪ Any attack severe enough to warrant treatment can qualify for treatment with icatibant 30 mg SC▪ Hospitalization for 5 hours, with follow-up on Day 2 and Day 14 and long term follow-up by telephone every 4 months and clinic visit every 6 months▪ Repeated dosing• If attack worsens within 48 hours of initial treatment, additional injections permitted• Maximum of 3 injections per attack at least 6 hours apart▪ Duration: 1 year after closure of double-blind phase• Efficacy variables○ Primary: Time to onset of symptom relief (cutaneous swelling, skin pain, abdominal pain, and nausea) by patient assessed Visual Analogue Scale (VAS, 10 cm line)▪ If pre-dose VAS \geq 30 mm, absolute reduction from pre-treatment VAS of \geq 20 mm▪ If pre-dose VAS &gt; 50 mm, absolute reduction from pre-treatment VAS of \geq 30 mm○ Laryngeal edema▪ Symptom score (0-4, none to very severe) of dysphagia, voice▪ Investigator to score abdominal tenderness, breathing difficulties, stridor, and asphyxia in addition○ Secondary▪ Response rate at 4 hours▪ Time to relief of each symptom present in pre-dose VAS other than the primary symptoms▪ Time to almost complete symptom relief (0-10 mm on VAS)▪ Investigator Global Assessment

L2418: ○ Safety
            
▪ N=35 (97.5%) had injection site reactions (erythema, swelling, warmth, burning, cutaneous pain, and itching) lasting ~ 3.5 hours
▪ HAE attacks
▪ Nasopharyngitis, upper and urinary tract infections also reported more commonly in the icatibant treatment group
▪ 1 death reported (sudden cardiac death attributed to aortic valve scleroris; 41 days after receipt of TA)
L2419: Reviewer's comment: Blinding of study treatments was incomplete, as is acknowledged by the Applicant. Subcutaneous injection of icatibant is associated with a high rate of local injection site reactions, presumably secondary to a pH buffer that is not included in the placebo injection. Oral tranexamic acid is known to cause nausea, vomiting, and diarrhea, in addition to a distinctive adverse effect of color vision disturbances. Since the primary and secondary endpoints are subjective, self-reported outcomes, the blinding issue is more prominent. Aside from the blinding issue, the lack of a separate placebo arm is another review issue. Tranexamic acid is not approved for HAE in the US, and data to support its efficacy for this indication has not been formally evaluated. As a result, any observed benefit of icatibant over tranexamic acid may be due to tranexamic acid worsening the patient's condition, rather than improvement attributable to icatibant, i.e. tranexamic acid may be worse than placebo.
L2420: Study 2103 (FAST-1)
L2421: • Dates: December 28, 2004 to July 17, 2007 (OLE ongoing)• Design○ Randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study○ Open-label study for laryngeal edema attacks○ Open-label extension• Objective: Assess the efficacy of icatibant compared with placebo on the onset of relief of symptoms resulting from moderate to very severe acute cutaneous and abdominal edema HAE attacks• Treatments○ Icatibant 30 mg SC injection (divided into 3 1-cc injections)○ Placebo SC injection• Patients○ 18 years of age and older○ Documented diagnosis of HAE Type I or Type II (medical history and/or functional C1-INH &lt;50% normal)
L2422: Reviewer's comment: Although both functional C1-INH and C4 levels were collected, the inclusion criteria were based only on the C1-INH level. C1q levels were not obtained for the study and were not routinely documented. Although a diagnosis of acquired angioedema (AAE) is an exclusion criterion, it is possible that patients with AAE who have previously been presumed to have HAE may have been enrolled in this study. Furthermore, the listing of protocol violations shows that 9 patients were granted study entry waivers on the basis of historical data and confirmed diagnosis of HAE, despite unavailable lab results or C1-INH levels higher than allowed. Of note, this protocol was previously reviewed under SPA in the Division of GI and Coagulation Drug Products (Medical Officer reviews – May 11, 2004 and July 28, 2004; SPA response letter – July 20, 2004) prior to transfer to DPAP in August 2005. Prior reviews and discussions did not address this aspect of the inclusion criteria.
L2423: • Conduct○ Double-blind treatment phase▪ Initial screening visit for eligibility▪ Patient presentation with moderate-to-severe acute HAE attack no later than 6 hours after the time the current attack becomes moderately severe▪ Hospitalization for minimum of 12 hours with study drug administration and follow-up 24 hours post-dose, Day 14▪ Symptom scoring every 30 min for first 4 hours, then at Hours 3, 6, 8, 10, 12, 24▪ Rescue medications to be avoided if possible; if used, recorded in the CRF○ Open-label treatment for laryngeal attacks○ Open-label extension
L2424: ▪ Open to all patients who have completed either double-blind treatment phase or open-label treatment for laryngeal attack
▪ Also open to patients who did not have an attack sufficiently severe enough to qualify for the double-blind treatment phase or have their 1st attack after the 56th patient has completed the double-blind treatment phase (Modified OLE)
▪ Repeat injections
            
• If attack worsens within 48 hours of treatment, may have repeated doses
• Maximum of 3 doses per attack at least 6 hours apart
• No more than 8 doses within any 4-week period


▪ Regular visits every 3 months in addition to unscheduled visits for acute attacks
▪ Additional follow-up on Day 2 and 14 for each new attack treated with icatibant

• Efficacy
            
○ Primary endpoint: time to onset of symptom relief
                    
▪ Absolute reduction by \geq 20 mm observed at 3 consecutive measurements


○ Secondary endpoints
                    
▪ Response rate at 4 hours
▪ Time to relief of nausea, vomiting, diarrhea in the abdominal group and the symptom less severe in the cutaneous group
▪ Time to almost complete symptom relief
▪ Global Assessment
▪ Assessment of each symptom by severity (0-5, not present to very severe)




• Results

○ Efficacy
L2425: Study 2103: Median time to onset of symptom relief (h)

Icatibant (n=27)Placebo (n=29)P



All attacks (n=55)2.54.60.142

Cutaneous (n=27)3.410.00.221

Abdominal (n=28)2.03.00.159

Males, all attacks (n=19)2.723.00.160

Females, all attacks (n=36)2.03.30.273
L2426: The Applicant reports that the median time to regression of symptoms as reported by the patient for laryngeal attacks (n=8) was 0.6 hours.
L2427: The Applicant has provided an alternate analysis excluding Study Site #7, citing inconsistencies in the classification of patient attack locations. Study Site #7 treated 10 patients in the double-blind treatment phase, as well as 1 laryngeal attack patient. The Applicant has also included another post-hoc analysis using a more stringent definition of onset of symptom relief (“any value to the right and below a line Y=4/7X - 50/7 with X = 30 mm”). Based on this analysis, the median time to onset of symptom relief is 2.5 versus 8.1 hours for icatibant and placebo, respectively (p=0.058).
L2428: Reviewer’s comment: While the additional analyses may be used as supportive sensitivity analyses, the primary review will focus on the ITT population. The study report does not provide the median time to onset of symptom relief for laryngeal patients. Combined with results of Study 2102, efficacy support for the use of icatibant in laryngeal HAE is based on a total of 11 patients, with additional supportive data from the open-label extension portions of each of the Phase 3 studies.
L2429: • Safety○ The most common adverse events was injection site reaction (n=26, 96.3%) as noted in Study 2102. Other adverse events reported more commonly in the icatibant group was nasopharyngitis, upper respiratory and urinary tract infections. The other common adverse event was HAE attack.
L2430: Reviewer’s comment: The Applicant states that “HAE attack” may have been over-reported with patients and investigators mistakenly identifying new attacks as AEs. HAE was only to be reported as an AE in the study protocol if these were laryngeal attacks or if there was “relevant worsening of an attack treated with study drug.”
L2431: 6. BRIEF REVIEW OF PROPOSED LABELING
L2432: Proposed package labeling has been included in this submission [1.14]. The Applicant seeks an indication for the treatment of hereditary angioedema.
L2433: 1. The efficacy and safety studies conducted in support of this application addressed the treatment of acute attacks of HAE specifically, not a general treatment indication.2. The Dosage and Administration describes the subcutaneous use of icatibant, but does not specify that injections should be administered by a trained health professional in a monitored setting, i.e. not for home use.3. The Adverse Reactions section includes data pooled from Phase 2 and 3 studies.4. Under Clinical Studies, the proposed label (b) (4)
L2434: 5. Although numerically supportive, Study 2103, the single placebo-controlled study in the application, did not demonstrate a statistically significant benefit for icatibant over placebo.
L2435: 6. The Clinical Studies section presents the result (b) (4)
L2436: A more extensive review of the product label is to follow.
L2437: Reviewer's comments: The proposed label follows the new content and format requirements.
L2438: 7. DSI REVIEW/AUDIT
L2439: The Applicant states that no debarred investigators participated in the study, and all investigators certified to having no financial conflicts of interest. Preliminary review of the data does not show any treatment-center effects. Since icatibant is a new molecular entity, a DSI audit is recommended. Because there are no financial conflicts with investigators or treatment-center effects, A DSI audit is recommended for the study sites which enrolled the most patients in Studies 2102 and 2103, respectively.
L2440: 8. PEDIATRIC ASSESSMENT
L2441: As Orphan Drug designation has been granted for icatibant, the Pediatric Research Equity Act of 2003 does not apply. The submission does not include any studies in pediatric subjects. The Applicant states that pediatric studies will be addressed pending approval in the adult population, as discussed during prior pre-NDA meetings (Meeting Minutes – March 1, 2005 and Meeting Minutes – February 22, 2007).
L2442: 9. ADVISORY COMMITTEE
L2443: As icatibant is a new molecular entity and is under evaluation for a novel indication, a Pulmonary and Allergy Drugs Advisory Committee Meeting has been tentatively scheduled for February 20, 2007. The discussion will focus on requirements for demonstrating efficacy and general safety.
L2444: 10. SUMMARY
L2445: This is a 45-day filing and planning review of an NDA for icatibant, a selective competitive antagonist at the bradykinin type 2 (B2) receptor. The Applicant, Jerini AG, has developed icatibant 30 mg subcutaneous injection for acute, intermittent use. The proposed indication is the treatment of hereditary angioedema (HAE). Two pivotal efficacy and safety studies are submitted in support of this application (JE049 2102 and 2103). The Applicant has provided additional data from several uncontrolled studies, including an open-label Phase 2 study of intravenously and subcutaneously administered icatibant in HAE patients (Study 2101) and pharmacokinetic studies in healthy volunteers (Studies 1101, 1102, and 1103). Safety information on repeated, long-term use is presented from the ongoing open-label extension studies for Studies 2102 and 2103 (2102-B and 2103-B, respectively). The Applicant has also provided results of a non-interventional study, 4102, to demonstrate the validity of the patient-completed Visual
L2446: Analogue Scale (VAS), which was used as the primary efficacy parameter in the Phase 3 studies. These study reports are appropriately indexed and organized to allow review. The Applicant has provided an Integrated Summary of Efficacy, Integrated Summary of Safety, Integrated Summary of Benefits and Risks, copies of proposed labeling, and appropriate case report forms.
L2447: The submission is adequate to allow clinical review. The submission is fileable.
L2448: 11. COMMENTS TO THE APPLICANT
L2449: The following comment will be conveyed to the Applicant in the 74-day filing letter regarding potential review issues:
L2450: 1. As discussed in the January 24, 2007, Pre-NDA meeting, you do not have two confirmatory studies demonstrating the efficacy of icatibant for the proposed indication. Of the two Phase 3 studies included in your NDA submission, the first study, Study 2102, used an active comparator which is not approved for the treatment of HAE in the United States and, the second study, Study 2103, did not demonstrate a statistically significant benefit of icatibant over placebo for the primary efficacy endpoint, time to onset of symptom relief. The adequacy of your data to support the efficacy of icatibant for the proposed indication will be a review issue.2. The proposed indication, treatment of hereditary angioedema, is broad and does not reflect your clinical development program. The efficacy and safety studies conducted in support of this application addressed the treatment of acute attacks of HAE specifically, not a general treatment indication.3. On the carton and container labels, remove the graphic next to the proprietary name as it detracts from the proprietary name (21 CFR 201.15(a)(6)) and decreases the relative prominence of the established name (21 CFR 201.10(g)(2)).
L2451: The following information will be requested from the Applicant in the 74-day filing letter:
L2452: 1. Define the following abbreviations used in the Patient Data Listings (Section 17.2) in the JE049 #2103 Clinical Study Report: NA, ND, NK, and UNK.2. Provide the median time to onset of symptom relief for laryngeal attack patients in Studies 2102 and 2103.3. Provide samples of the drug product for our review.
